Preface
|
| The importance of Financial Statements is well known. These statements
i convey the performance achieved and risk inherent in an enterprise and
i help assess its future earnings and cash flow prospects. It is vital for business
managers, professionals, exccutives, and aspiring directors to not only grasp the
information conveyed by income statements, cash flow statements, and balance
sheet but also use the information in their careers.

This book is for management professionals, executive programme
participants, and business school students keen to learn about financial statements
prepared following Ind AS, the IFRS-based new accounting standards in India.
With the help of the book, one can comprehend the contents of financial
statements and analyze them to assess the operating and overall performance,
efficiency, liquidity, and solvency of Indian companies.

“This book has several outstanding features as listed below:

First, this book does not require any prior accounting and finance
knowledge.

«Second, we discuss the concepts in brief and simple language and
emphasize their importance and application.

Third, this book has under 200 pages that can be quickly covered.

Fourth, the chapters present the essential material, whereas appendices
have advanced materials, which can be skipped.

Fifth, with the help of a mini case, the book explains in detail how
transactions affect financial statements and how one can easily prepare
financial statements from transactions.

This book has five chapters. Chapter 1 covers the fundamental concepts
helpful in understanding financial statements. Italso has a mini-case, illustrating

| the effect of transactions on financial statements and the preparation of financial
statements. The following three chapters are for understanding and analysis of
income statement (Chapter 2), balance sheet (Chapter 3), and cash flow statement

© Key Financial Ratios Reported by Infosys 186 FI Business & Activities
A business is an economic unit that invests, lends money, or sells goods and
services to customers to generate an adequate rate of return for its owners."
Business owners start and operate a business to serve customer needs for a price
and to make a profit. To achieve their goals, firms transact with various parties.
A transaction is an exchange of money, goods, or services which affects the firm's
financial position. Financial position changes due to a change in assets (resources
a firm controls), liabilities (the firm's obligations), and equities (owner's claim on
| assets).
| A transaction can arise from financing, investing, or operating activity.
Firms raise funds (financing activities). They use the funds to acquire fixed assets
(investing activities) and working capital (operating activities) needed to stare
the operations. They carry out procurement, production, sales, service delivery,
and administration of the business (operating activities).
"To vary exposition, the terms — business, firm, entity, enterprise, company, and corporation —
are used synonymously throughout this book to refer to a for-profic company. Any legal
differences among these terms have been ignored.

(f) Income Tax Expense (g) Profit After Tax (PAT)
Income Tax Expense arises from direct taxes on corporate profits. There are two | Profit After Tax is the profit available to shareholders. It is known by various
components of Income Tax Expense: Current Tax and Deferred Tax. x i : x ;
2 | names, including Net earnings, Net income, Net profit, Net profit after tax,
Tax Expense = Current Tie + Deferred Tax | Profit for the year, and simply, Profit. It is also informally referred to as the
Current Tax Expense is the amount of tax payable during a year computed | bottom line. It represents the aggregate performance of a firm from operating,
as per income tax rules. Deferred Tax Expense is the difference between income investing, and financing activities. A company earns net income after meeting all
tax expense and current tax expense. Deferred taxes arise due to temporary expenses (including taxes) from total income. Thus, it is the total income net of
differences between PBT (obtained using accounting rules) and Taxable Income total expenses. A company can pay dividends out of its net profit of the current
(obtained using tax rules), which arise from differences in accounting rules and and previous years (or retained earnings).
tax rules. As temporary differences eventually reverse, so do deferred taxes. See In 2021-22, the net profit reported by Asian Paints was 33,135 crore

financial assets. Examples of non-financial assets are inventory, PPE, intangible

(advances to procure a PPE) and CWIP will result in PPE. PPE is held for the
running of the enterprise or for use in the production of goods. Raw material

313-2022. Avenue Supermarts’ capital exiployed BRLip0 shi wa iy Biological assets are living animals and plants. Biological assets are classified into
equity 713,924 crore and debt R416 crore. Infosys employed capital of 373, |" bearer plants (included under PPE) and biological assets other than bearer plants
crore consisting of equity ¥69,306 crore and debe 33,786 crore. | (included under current assets).

According to Ind AS 16 (Property, Plant, and Equipment), bearer plants
are living plants that are (i) used in the production or supply of agricultural |
produce, (ii) expected to bear produce for more than one period, and (iii) are |
remotely likely to be sold as agricultural produce except for incidental scrap
sales. Thus, bearer plants are no: (i) annual crops (such as rice or wheat),

(ii) plants cultivated to be harvested as agricultural produce (such as trees
grown as lumber), and (iii) plants, although cultivated primarily for agricultural
produce, may also be harvested and sold (for example, trees cultivated for their |
fruits and lumber). Although they are biological assets, Bearer Plants related to

| agriculture activities are classified as PPE following Ind AS 16 (Property, Plant,
and Equipment). Therefore, these assets are accounted for as self-constructed
PPE and reported at their net block. |

As on 31-3-2022, Dhunseri Tea & Industries Ltd. had Bearer Plants I
(Gross Block ¥126 crore, Accumulated Depreciation 14 crore, Net Block
113 crore) included in PPE as per note 3a. Note that Asian Paints, Avenue
Supermarts, and Infosys are not from the agriculture industry, and their PPE |
does not include any bearer plants.

Biological Assets other than Bearer Plants include (i) produce from orchards |
and plantations such as fruits, seeds, leaves, latex, and lumber, (ii) annual crops |
such as rice, wheat, lentils, and vegetables, (iii) floriculture such as flowers,

(iv) aquaculture such as fish and shrimps, (v) livestock such as dairy, wool, and
meat, (vi) poultry such as eggs and meat, etc. These assets are accounted for

following Ind AS 41 (Agriculture) and are reported similar to inventory under

current assets at fair value less any cost to sell.

As on 31-3-2022, Dhunseri Tea & Industries Ltd. had Biological Assets
other than Bearer Plants of 372 lakh (2.49 lakh kg) reported under Current |
Assets. Another company, Kaveri Seed reported Biological Assets (comprising |
standing crops) of I82 crore under Current Assets on its Balance Sheet as at |
March 31, 2022. Again, Asian Paints, Avenue Supermarts, and Infosys did not |
have any biological assets other than bearer plants.

impairment loss reversals should be treated as non-operating in nature. Assets
1. Cash and Balances with the Reserve Bank of India (RBI), which includes
| cash in hand and balances with the RBI in current account and other
accounts.
| 2. Balances with Banks and money at call and short notice, which are similar
| to cash equivalents. Banks earn interest income on these amounts.
3. Investments, which includes investments made in government and
other approved securities, shares, bonds, debentures, mutual funds, and
investments in subsidiaries, associates, and joint-ventures. Banks earn
interest and dividend income on investments.
4. Advances, which includes long-term loans, short-term loans, cash
credits, overdrafts, and bills purchased and discounted. Banks earn
s interest income on advances.
5. Fixed Assets, which is similar in nature to Property, Plant, and
Equipment, Capital Work-in-Progress, and Intangible Assets.
6. Other Assets, which includes all other assets not included in the first five
categories.
Capital and Liabilities
1. Capital, which is similar to Equity Share Capital.
2. Reserves and Surplus, which is similar in nature to Other Equity.
3. Deposits, which comprises demand, savings, and term deposits
received.

India (RBI), other banks, other institutions and agencies, and capital

|
| (a) Introduction Stage
In this stage, companies focus on raising money from financing activities and
deploying them to build capacity and fund cash needed in operating activities.
The 2021-22 Cash Flow Statement of Zomato reveals negative CFOA (509)
crore, negative CFIA (8,131) crore, and positive CFFA 8,763 crore. The pattern
indicates that Zomato is in the introduction phase of the company lifecycle. |
| Note that Zomato received interest of T114 crore and paid total interest of T(4)
crore, which are small in magnitude and, therefore, if we obtained the amounts
as per the US GAAP, we would continue to make a similar inference. Another
example of a company that shows a similar pattern of cash flows from operating,
investing, and financing activities is One 97 Communications (known for the
PayTM brand).

(c) Mature Stage Sign of net CFFA (US GAAP) + = N |
Mature companies generate large amounts of cash from operating activities, use
these funds to replace fixed assets and build investments (net cash outflows from
investing activities) and pay dividends, buyback of shares, repay borrowings, etc.
(net cash outflows from financing activities).
We infer that Asian Paints, Avenue Supermarts, and Infosys are in the
mature stage of their lifecycle based on the signs of cash flows from operating, |
investing, and financing sections as per Indian GAAP (i.c., without doing the
conversion to US GAAP). Had we done the conversion, we would still reach the
same conclusion since the adjustments required are relatively small in magnitude. |
